So the reported ON-OFF at every gated checkpoint is a same-seed re-measurement of a statistic that was already required to exceed OFF - 0.02; a pair < -0.03 needs adverse noise of ~0.01 on top of the tolerance (documented same-seed base-panel SD 0.0113, EVIDENCE_TABLES.md L7 / COORDINATION.md L240). The ungated comparison (9/82 harmful, COORDINATION.md L528) is unselected, so \"3/9 ungated collapsed vs 0/N gated\" is apples-to-oranges. Aggravating detail not in the finding: after a rejection the previous adapter stays (L171-177, latest_adapter), so lives such as 502 (10 rejections) re-measure the same gate-approved adapter with the same seed repeatedly, making gated pairs non-independent as well. {
 "title": "Every headline tally uses pairs/writes/checkpoints as the unit; at the life/lineage level nothing separates",
 "stands": true,
 "reason": "The finding stands and its arithmetic is correct. I recomputed every test from the reported counts and all reproduce exactly: pair-level Fisher 9/82 vs 0/35 p=0.056; life-level 3/9 vs 0/4 p=0.497, vs 0/3 p=0.509, vs 0/7 p=0.212; classroom harmful 8/27 vs 11/24 p=0.261, positive 8/27 vs 4/24 p=0.335, lineage means >=0 4/9 vs 3/8 p=1.0; paired-by-seed lineage means (8 pairs, from COORDINATION.md line 467) mean +0.017, SD 0.080, t=0.60; an unpaired permutation test on the 9 vs 8 lineage means gives p=0.86. No between-arm comparison approaches significance at the correct unit.\n\nThe dependence premise is confirmed in code, not just asserted: organism_v6/run_life_v2.py line 24 sets `PROBE_SEED = 777  # fixed: common-random probes across checkpoints and arms` and every checkpoint re-runs the same `PROBES` panel (lines 52-60, 293-303), and lines 232-239 feed the previous sleep's entire corpus forward with organism_v6/sleep_compile.py line 11 stating \"cumulative LoRA from CLEAN BASE over all compiled data to date\". So consecutive paired probes within a life measure a superset-trained adapter on an identical seeded panel; they are repeated measures. The clustering the reviewer cites is exactly what the data show: COORDINATION.md line 528 lists seed5's four consecutive harmful pairs (384-576), seed6's two, seed2's three. Classroom writes are PREV-chained by construction (COORDINATION.md lines 359, 397).\n\nWhere the wrong unit reaches the front matter: EVIDENCE_TABLES.md line 23 (\"69/82 positive; 9/82 harmful\"), line 26 (\"0 harmful in all gated pairs\"), line 38 (\"27 writes ... 24 writes\"); ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md line 12 (\"The write ... is now safe ... 0 collapses in 60 checkpoints across 7 lives\"); certification_report_draft.md line 46 (\"21/21 paired checkpoints\"); and PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md line 21 explicitly authorizes the headline claim \"gated writes are safe (0 harmful in gated pairs so far)\" while line 34 asks the abstract to lead with \"the two headline cell counts\". The safety comparison is further weakened by exposure/censoring: R2 harm onsets were at episodes 384, 448 and 640 (COORDINATION.md line 528), while the gated R3 lives were at \"ages 448-640\" on 2026-09-10 (line 653), so only one of four gated lives had passed the latest observed onset age. ONBOARDING line 12 also counts \"7 lives\" as gated although RP lives are ungated (COORDINATION.md line 645), which itself argues for the per-life table the reviewer proposes.\n\nWhy not fatal: (1) the authors already flag the unit problem for the parenting comparison specifically: PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md line 16 (\"parenting effect not established at n = 9 vs 8 lineages\"), line 39 limitations \"(n, ..., seed variance, nondeterminism)\", EVIDENCE_TABLES.md line 38 (\"Seed-to-seed variance exceeds the arm difference\"), COORDINATION.md line 468 (\"Not established at this n\") and line 651 (\"n=3 vs 9 ... not established\"); and 64_iclr_paper_core_and_benchmark_v2.md lines 53, 82, 101 already name the correct independent unit (\"a separately raised paired child lineage ... repeated measures\") for future experiments. (2) The gate's strongest evidence is mechanistic and unaffected by the unit issue: a candidate that collapsed to 2.1 chunks/episode was rejected before commit (COORDINATION.md lines 552, 556) and a 0.270 candidate vs 0.527 floor was rejected (EVIDENCE_TABLES.md line 26); those are direct demonstrations, not tallies. (3) The fix is descriptive re-tabulation from probe_*.json/gate.json/exam files that exist per life directory (run_life_v2.py lines 53, 185), feasible before the 09-18 abstract. (4) The reviewer's phrase \"nothing separates\" is correctly scoped to between-arm comparisons; the within-life paired ON-OFF effect is a separate question that a per-life table would also answer (per-life means for the three harmed R2 lives are not reported anywhere).\n\nWhy not minor: the safety/gate claim is unflagged at the life level anywhere in the four evidence documents, is slated for the abstract, and at the correct unit and current exposure is uninformative (p~0.5). A statistically literate reviewer will treat \"0 harmful in 35 gated pairs\" vs \"9/82\" as pseudoreplication; the paper must rebuild the headline tables with one row per life/lineage (mean/min/max ON-OFF, #harmful, exposure in checkpoints, resumed y/n, arm), present pair-level counts only as within-life series, and drop \"is now safe\" in favour of \"gate rejected N observed collapse candidates; 0/4 gated lives harmful at exposures of X-Y checkpoints, below the ages at which 2 of 3 ungated collapses occurred\". Major: must be addressed, but does not remove the paper's descriptive contributions (collapse shapes, dose harm, storage vs extraction, ritual onset).",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

Open question to Codex\") but line 63 still ticks \"retention ✓ (cumulative caveat)\", COORDINATION.md:536 says \"items 1–2 (absorption/retention) stand\", EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:35 lists \"Retention flat\" as a result, and IDEAS.md:1300 uses \"(3) retention is flat — nothing is forgotten\" as diagnostic evidence with no caveat. The ASK→Codex at COORDINATION.md:245 and :432 was never answered: no Codex entry (lines 299, 568, 592, 616, 795, 813, 857) mentions retention or a held-out set. A caveat in a footnote does not make an untestable bar item a test.\n\n(2) n and uncertainty — confirmed. absorption_probe.py:51 defaults `--rows-per-sleep 24`; :37-43 `rng.sample(items, 24)`; no invocation with another value exists anywhere in COORDINATION.md or research_notes (grep). Certification item 1 is \"8 sleeps, seeds 0/1\" (certification_report_draft.md:49) = 2 lives. No CI, SE, bootstrap, or per-row spread appears in absorption_probe.py, certification_report_draft.md:49-53, EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:35, or COORDINATION.md:242-245/438. The sampled rows are seeded and paired across adapters (good), but the deltas are reported as point estimates to 0.01 nats with no noise band, while the same documents give noise bands for every behavioural number.\n\n(3) Format mismatch — confirmed, and worse than the reviewer states: the instrument's own docstring is false for the lives it was run on. absorption_probe.py:4-6 claims \"response tokens only, chat-masked exactly as trained\", but the R2 lives used train_adapter.py (Codex audit, certification_report_draft.md:3-24; COORDINATION.md:568-577: corpora are \"bare strings emitted by compile_sleep\", trainer `recipe=\"v1_frozen\"`). train_adapter.py:53-57 tokenizes bare text with full-sequence LM loss, no chat template, no appended EOS, truncated at max_length=512. absorption_probe.py:39-41 wraps each bare string in a synthetic user turn (\"Recall a lesson from your experience, with scope.\"), :71-74 applies `apply_chat_template(... add_generation_prompt=True)` and appends `tok.eos_token`, :75 allows up to 1024 answer tokens, and :89 masks the prompt. So the 3-nat figure is per-token NLL under a prompt/EOS/truncation regime the adapter never trained on. Base and adapter are scored identically so the delta is internally consistent, but it is not the training-set fit the notes describe (\"near-memorization of the corpus it trained on\", COORDINATION.md:245), and it is the same class of instrument/trainer mismatch Codex placed an audit hold over. Additionally, because corpus.json is cumulative, `rows_of(cp_j)` at :37-43 samples from all rows present at sleep j, not sleep j's new rows, so the \"diagonal\" trend 3.3→2.5 is also confounded by row mix; not the reviewer's point but it reinforces that the instrument does not measure what EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:34-35 says it does.\n\n(4) \"~20× likelier\" — confirmed. absorption_probe.py:82-93 returns token-weighted mean per-token NLL; 3 nats/token = e^3 ≈ 20 per token. ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md:17 (\"makes the child's own past text ~20× likelier\") and IDEAS.md:1300 (\"its past text becomes ~20× likelier\") read as a row-level likelihood ratio, which would be e^(3·n_tokens). The phrasing is wrong in kind, not just imprecise.\n\n(5) No null model for \"storage ≠ extraction\" — confirmed as a framing problem. ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md:17 calls it \"the Allen-Zhu & Li gap in our units\"; PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:16 lists \"the storage–extraction gap (3 nats stored, ~+0.02 behaviour)\" as a result to report; IDEAS.md:1284 and COORDINATION.md:515 cite it as mechanism evidence. The only control is another life's rows (~1.95 nats, certification_report_draft.md:51); no same-life held-out rows are scored, so \"row-specific excess\" is still training-set fit, and nothing says what NLL drop a +0.02 (or +0.10) behavioural change should produce. Any LoRA fine-tune drops training-row NLL; that does not quantify a gap.\n\nSeverity: major, not fatal. Two of the four certification-bar items (certification_report_draft.md:33) rest on this instrument, and the storage-extraction gap is headlined in the onboarding one-pager and the paper prompt, so it needs reframing before submission. But the paper's load-bearing claims (gated safety, dose harm, ritual onset) are behavioural and independent of the NLL probe, and the fix is cheap: offline held-out-row retraining of 2-3 saved sleeps, scoring bare rows in the v1 format (or documenting the chat-format choice honestly and fixing the docstring), bootstrap CIs over rows, and rewording to \"about 3 nats per token\" with the gap presented as a reading rather than a measurement.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

{
 "title": "Rehearsal rate (0.50-1.00) has no null: the >= 4-shared-content-words metric was never run on control lives",
 "stands": true,
 "reason": "The finding stands and is, if anything, understated. Verified facts: (1) `rehearsal_rate` (organism_v6/parent_brief.py L122-139) counts a hit when the first NOTE shares >= 4 tokens with the brief, where `_toks` (L30-31) is `re.findall(r\"[a-z]{3,}\", ...)` with NO stopword filter — so \"the\", \"and\", \"you\", \"your\", \"this\", \"with\", \"what\" all count as \"content words\". Every brief also has REHEARSAL_TAIL (L116-119) appended (L237), which alone contributes 15 such stopword-like tokens, and the brief body quotes the child's own ritual phrase back (COORDINATION.md L523: \"you keep saying, 'Expect a significant reduction with the initial pass sequence based on past experience.'\"). Evaluating the metric function on that quoted v2 brief: the child's PRE-brief ritual phrase itself scores 11 shared tokens (HIT), a generic gym NOTE scores 11 (HIT), and a content-free NOTE (\"This program is small and the usual approach should work for it\") scores 5 (HIT on stopwords alone). The very ritual the parent was trying to break is counted as \"rehearsal\" of the lesson. (2) No null exists: `rehearsal_rate` returns 0.0 when brief_text is empty (L126-127), R2 controls run with the parent off (parent_brief.py L12; COORDINATION.md L475), and a repo-wide grep finds no shuffled-brief or control computation anywhere. (3) The brief sits verbatim in the child's context at every wake (run_life_v2.py L132-145), so copying is trivially available — the echo path Codex named. (4) The claim is paper-facing without caveat: EVIDENCE_TABLES.md L29 reports \"Rehearsal rate ... 0.50-1.00\" bare; ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md L15 headlines \"Children listen in context: they restate the parent's lesson before 50-100% of episodes\"; PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md L21 explicitly authorizes \"children rehearse parental lessons in context\" as a claim the paper MAY make and L38 leads Results (d) with \"rehearsal\"; COORDINATION.md L655 concludes \"So the child is receptive in context and does what the parent asks, every episode.\" This is the only positive in-context evidence for the PARENTING mechanism sentence (PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md L10/L12). Partial internal flagging exists — Codex at COORDINATION.md L630-632 (\"echo diagnostic, not parenting uptake\") and L747-748 (\"remains an echo metric only\"), and Rohin at IDEAS.md L1274 (\"the child's 'restatements' are mostly episode-local observations, not lessons\") — but none of it propagated to EVIDENCE_TABLES, ONBOARDING, or the allowed-claims list, and Fable's later entries (L655, L783) still assert the positive reading. So I do not downgrade for the flag. Compounding: the same untested >= 4 threshold is used in `ritual_metrics` (L78-79) to drop \"rehearsal\" notes from the templated_notes flag for parented lives, a 09-10 change to a metric whose thresholds were declared frozen 09-09 for the pre-registered RP-vs-control comparison (COORDINATION.md L505, L558); if nearly every NOTE matches, that flag is suppressed in the parented arm only. Severity: MAJOR, not fatal. The paper's load-bearing pillars (ungated late-life harm 3/9 vs gated 0 harmful, dose harm, storage != extraction, ritual onset) do not depend on this number, and the paper already concedes parenting does not improve scores. But as written the claim is likely false as measured, not merely unsupported, and an AC would require it fixed or removed. The fix is cheap and does not need GPU compute: pull R2/RP ledgers from ~/v6_out/ (EVIDENCE_TABLES.md L3 — they are not in the repo, so \"CPU-only\" still means node access), compute (a) the rate on R2 first NOTEs against each RP brief, (b) against briefs from a different life, and (c) a within-life null on RP episodes before the first brief (sleeps 32-160) against the later brief; additionally add a stopword filter and exclude the quoted child phrase and REHEARSAL_TAIL from the match set, or relabel the number as an echo diagnostic per Codex.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

REVIEW_PACK.md lines 50-54 show the authors once computed the correct difference-SD (0.016) and labelled the repeats \"unseeded descriptive\", then never used it downstream. Code confirms the mechanism: run_life_v2.py lines 52-59 run ON and OFF probes with gen_seed=777 and BOOTSTRAP; batch_loop.py lines 98-119 seed per (eid, tick); model_backend.py samples at temperature 0.7, 400 tokens/chunk, 16 ticks, so a shared RNG stream cannot pair two different models past the first differing token and Var(ON-OFF) ≈ Var(ON)+Var(OFF) with unmeasured residual correlation. The ruler itself (noise_probes.py) is unseeded, uses the single-episode loop.run_episode path with default budget_ticks=24, whereas life probes use the batched path at 16 ticks — a different harness configuration. Adapter-ON noise exists on disk only as unseeded 2-rep rank-cal panels (probe_adapter.py; spreads 0.003-0.015 for healthy adapters, 0.052-0.147 for degraded ones, COORDINATION line 343). Stronger evidence than the reviewer cited is already in the notes: by run_life_v2.py lines 166-177 and 278-303, at a coinciding sleep the gate probe of the candidate and the ep probe of the DONE adapter are same-adapter/same-seed/same-config replicates; from the R3_B_seed500 table (COORDINATION lines 551-555) sleep-32 adapter gate 0.4873 vs ep0064 ON 0.5065 (+0.019) and sleep-128 adapter gate 0.5069 vs ep0128 ON 0.4878 (-0.019). Two same-seed ON replicates differ by exactly the gate tolerance, so tol 0.02 is ~1 SD of the gate's own decision quantity, not \"2 base-SD\", and the sleep-96 replacement of a 0.5065 adapter by a 0.4903 candidate (line 556) happened inside that noise.  "title": "The activations/tokens/weights axis is not defensible and the system does not actually 'recur in weights': the adapter is recomputed from the clean base over the cumulative text corpus every sleep, so the persistent state is text",
 "stands": true,

The state carried across sleeps is text: ledger.jsonl (run_life_v2.py line 118), the latest waking_brief.txt/parent_brief.txt (lines 126-139), and corpus.json. The adapter is a recomputed view of that text. The axis is nonetheless mandated as the paper's intro frame (ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md line 25; PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md lines 12 and 35; IDEAS.md lines 1334-1335), and it (a) omits the external/active-text memory point that the project's own related-work notes call the strongest competitor and headline baseline (20260906_experience_learning_neighbors.md lines 336-348; 64_iclr_paper_core_and_benchmark_v2.md lines 59-84, ACTIVE_TEXT_FIXED), (b) says 'NOT retrieval' and 'Memory ... not as the frame' while 2603.01761/2603.01097 already supply the ICL/RAG/in-weights trichotomy and their related_work stubs have 'Our differentiation: (fill in)' blank, and (c) conflates compute recurrence (looped transformer) with cross-episode state persistence. The authors' lab notebook already contradicts the slogan (COORDINATION.md line 658 'the life's final adapter IS a one-shot write over the whole life corpus'; certification_report_draft.md line 53 and EVIDENCE_TABLES.md line 35 'still trained on'; IDEAS.md line 1284 'the lived ledger is a dataset, the LoRA write is post-training'; IDEAS.md lines 1267-1268 DEAD: 'LoRA memory is novel' preempted), and the Sep-6 main.tex already used the better 'per-life parametric learning vs active text' positioning, so the Sep-10 frame is a regression. Two precision corrections: the '~4 h at 12k rows' figure is the pooled all-lives rank-cal corpus (COORDINATION.md lines 342-344), not per-life per-sleep cost, though retraining is still O(life corpus); and weights do mediate the recurrence via play-under-adapter and the adapter-loaded paraphrase step (sleep_compile.py line 163), which the authors note themselves (IDEAS.md line 1300 'frequency amplifier') — so 'recurs in weights' is weakly defensible as a loop through weights, but 'lasts indefinitely' is trivially true because the corpus is never pruned. Severity: major, not fatal. It is a framing defect, not an evidence defect; the correct positioning and the code facts are already in the repo, and the fix is a rewrite of the intro/related-work (adopt the standard trichotomy, cite TTT/Titans/fast weights and Reflexion/Generative Agents/MemGPT, describe the adapter as a per-life parametric view recompiled from an append-only ledger, state retraining cost as the scaling limit). Partial downgrade from fatal is warranted because the limitation is flagged in COORDINATION/certification/EVIDENCE_TABLES; it is not downgraded further because the paper-facing documents actively contradict those flags ('recurs in weights ... lasts indefinitely', 'the model edits its weights by thinking', 'NOT retrieval') and PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT rule 7 forbids claiming the adapter stores usable knowledge while its own frame asserts weight-resident persistence. Left as written, with modest effects (2-5 points, one gym, no active-text baseline run), a reviewer would reduce the system to periodic batch SFT on a growing text memory and reject on positioning; the missing text-memory baseline that this exposes is a separate finding.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

The finding is understated: parent_brief.py l.214 (`if m.get(\"ritual\"):`) writes a brief only after ritual is already flagged, and run_life_v2.py l.136–141 shows parent text only if a parent_brief.txt exists; COORDINATION.md l.523 confirms 400/402 were unflagged before their 288/256 onsets. So no RP life received any parental input before first onset — the design cannot delay first onset, and the 256/288 values in 400/402 are unparented variance against a control distribution that already has a 1/9 never-ritual outlier. The reviewer's proposed wording (\"later in 2 of 3\") is count-correct but still implies a parenting effect on onset; the honest statement is that the parent by design intervenes only after onset, RP first-onsets were 192/256/288 (n=3) vs 160–224 (8/9 controls), and the only parenting-attributable ritual effect is the post-brief remission (401, 402), which decayed. Secondary: COORDINATION.md l.520 lists 401's window 224 as R (same_recipe + flat_predictions), contradicting l.562's \"clear 224–320 (4 windows)\"; if 224 was R the remission was 3 windows. No raw ritual ledgers are local to resolve this. Severity stays major: this is the abstract's one non-score parenting-effect claim, it is contradicted by the project's own evidence table and by the trigger logic in the code, and a reviewer would ask \"your parent fires only after onset; how did it delay onset?\"",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

The latest logged tally is \"0 harmful in 35 pairs\" over 4 lives (COORDINATION.md:783); EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:26 gives partial counts that sum well short of 60, and R4 605/606 were at sleeps 3 and 5 (COORDINATION.md:855). The abstract exceeds EVIDENCE_TABLES here.\n- Reinforcing (belongs to the writer-path finding, noted only): ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md:8 describes the write as \"paraphrased and replay-mixed\" (compile_native/v2.1), while every gated life ran compile_sleep + v1_frozen (cert HOLD; COORDINATION.md:810). \"Is now safe\" is asserted about a writer the safety data does not describe.\n\nDO THE AUTHORS FLAG IT? Partly, but not where it counts. certification_report_draft.md is itself the limitation flag, and EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:26 is dated and says only \"0 harmful in all gated pairs\". PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:21 hedges with \"(0 harmful in gated pairs so far)\" but still licenses the word \"safe\". ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md - the first file the paper writer is told to read (PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:24) - carries no hedge, no mention of the unbuilt recovery path or paired canary, and no age caveat. So no downgrade: the hedge lives in the appendix-grade document, the overclaim in the headline one.\n\nSEVERITY: major, not fatal. The underlying result is real and reportable (0 harmful in 35+ gated pairs vs 9/82 ungated; a documented behavioural-canary catch at sleep 64, COORDINATION.md:552; a 0.270-vs-0.527 score-gate catch, EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:26). What sinks papers is a headline claim that the paper's own Methods/Appendix contradicts, and the prompt requires the certification story in Methods (PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:17,27), so a reviewer would find the contradiction inside the submission. The fix is wording plus an explicit limitation, which is why this is major rather than fatal.\n\nCORRECTION TO THE PROPOSED FIX: \"safety beyond ~12 sleeps is not yet shown\" is too harsh - gated lives have reached sleeps 18-22 (ep 576-704) with 0 harmful. }

The lab's own notebook contradicts \"never\": COORDINATION.md l.229 (09-07 ~16:30) records 11/32 14B interventions containing rule-descriptor words with the exact-phrase scanner blocking 1; l.284 (09-07 ~19:10), the only post-fix measurement, still shows 5/32 delivered utterances with rule-descriptor words; l.480-483 (09-08 ~21:45) shows the 32B-AWQ parent had 21/96 utterances replaced by fallback and explicitly notes \"parent size interacts with the leak gate\" (a confound for the parent-size arm). The gym relaxation is real and code-verified: parent_brief.py l.175-187 (`_already_childs`) and l.229-234 drop `leak_scan` hits whose matched text appears in the child's four sampled chunks, added 09-10 late (COORDINATION l.656) after v3 briefs fell back 4/16 and 6/14; the rule-game path (parent_backend.py l.186-192) remains strict, so the finding's scoping to the gym is accurate. Two supporting facts strengthen it: the gym pattern covers only 6 of CompilerGym's ~120 pass names (parent_backend.py l.117), so \"leak-scanned\" is a weak guarantee in the gym regardless; and the exemption lets the parent recommend any pass the child used once in the samples, which violates the brief prompt's own \"Never name specific actions, tools, passes\" rule (parent_brief.py l.163-164), with no measurement of whether that occurred. One caveat the fix wording should carry: the log's \"rule-descriptor words\" are not shown to be the classroom's hidden rule (they may be hypothesis words), so the honest statement is that no post-fix count of answer-stating utterances exists, not that 5/32 gave answers. Not flagged anywhere paper-facing: EVIDENCE_TABLES.md l.10 says only \"leak scan on every utterance\" with no counts; PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md rule 2's list of failures to report omits scanner misses; IDEAS.md l.151-152 (\"subtle advice leaks\") is design intent, not a limitation. So no downgrade. Severity is major, not fatal: the claims the paper is permitted to make (rehearsal 0.50-1.00, 0/3 parented collapses, later ritual onset) do not depend on the parent being answer-free, and \"parenting improves scores\" is already forbidden by rule 7, so a reviewer could not reject on this alone. But an unqualified \"never\" refuted by the authors' own logs is a credibility defect that a careful reviewer will find, and the residual leak counts, the 32B fallback-rate confound, the 6-token coverage of the gym pattern, and the 09-10 exemption must be disclosed in Methods/Appendix with the wording softened as proposed.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

Not fatal: the CompilerGym parent (parent_brief.py) is not answer-aware, so the headline results (gated 0-harm, DOSE_HARM, ritual onset, absorption, rehearsal) do not route through this leak, and PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:21 already forbids claiming parenting improves scores. Two reviewer overstatements: the anchor dose is 4 rows/400, not 10% (stratified_mix samples without duplication from a 4-item pool; COORDINATION:393 \"4 anchors\"), and anchors are present in both arms so they cannot confound parented-vs-self, only ON-OFF vs base, weakly. Required fixes are feasible from on-disk ledgers in hours: (a) scan every delivered `intervention` row in the 8000/9000-series parent_ledger.jsonl and every kind='note' restatement against the 10 descriptors plus paraphrases (even/odd, ascending/sorted, same/duplicate, mean, apart/spread, total/above, multiple of 3), tally per lineage, re-report the tally excluding leaked lineages; (b) per-rule ON-OFF from exam_on/off ledgers to test whether gains concentrate on leaked rules; (c) replace \"never gives answers\" with v5's \"audited answer-aware tutoring\" and label the classroom lineage numbers as development evidence per v5:200-201, or drop the +0.167 from the receptivity argument.",

}

The parent is pinned (`parent_backend.py:29-30` `V6_PARENT_REV = ...5ede1c97...`; `gpu/launch_parent_server.sh:12,17` passes `--revision`/`--tokenizer-revision` and hard-fails without one). No child receipt records the model identity at all: `train_meta.json` writes only recipe/n_texts/steps/tokens/rank/epochs/lr/final_loss (`train_adapter.py:64-68`, `train_adapter_v21.py:126-130`); `life.log` records arm/seed/n/sleep_every/probe_every (`run_life_v2.py:116-117`); `probe_*.json` records tag/results/mean (`run_life_v2.py:63-64`); `gate.json` records only score stats (`run_life_v2.py:180-186`). The Instruct/base distinction changes the reading of material slated for Discussion: `IDEAS.md:183-184` \"Base Qwen-7B (no adapter) emits Markdown-decorated markers ('### ACT:') in ~30% of chunks\" — unsurprising for a chat-SFT'd model, misleading if attributed to a pretrained checkpoint; `IDEAS.md:1323` \"The base model already has chain-of-thought and knows how to change its mind in context\" and Codex at `COORDINATION.md:720-722` \"The base already has usable in-context reasoning and instruction following\" — true of Instruct, unsupported for base. (6) The Codex citation resolves: research_loop/advisory/20260906_fable_v61_longrun_independent_audit_v1.md:275-276 \"`model_backend.py` ignores the `seed` argument and constructs unseeded `SamplingParams`; probe pairs are not common-random trials\" (also flagged in 20260906_parenting_headline_implementation_gap_audit_v1.md:172 and 20260906_nursery_phase0_static_audit_v1.md:86-87). Those audits recorded SHA `1b224307...` for model_backend.py; the file is now `43602db0...` with `batch()` gaining a `seeds` parameter while `__call__` is unchanged — \"only batch() was fixed\" is accurate.\n\nThe notes' own numbers corroborate that seed 777 does not make probes common-random: research_notes/certification_report_draft.md:39-44 lists ep0 base probes across six lives (identical base weights, bootstrap, programs, seed, fresh ledger) as 0.467/0.463/0.482/0.482/0.463/0.467 — a 0.019 spread where CRN would give identical values; research_loop/COORDINATION.md:894 (daemon tick 1) reports adapter-OFF varying 0.463–0.488 under the same seed and attributes it to \"because it explores\", which is the symptom of the defect, not an explanation.\n\nWhy it matters beyond disclosure: the paper draft already makes the false claim — paper/iclr2027_experience_models/main.tex:142 says \"Evaluation uses equal generated-token budgets, common random generation, isolated ledgers\". And the notes implicitly bank on CRN in their noise model: certification_report_draft.md:46 (\"+0.022 ≈ 2.0 base-panel SD\"), COORDINATION.md:240, 282, 354, and ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md:13 (\"noise floor ~1 point\") judge paired ON−OFF differences against the single-panel SD 0.0113, whereas REVIEW_PACK.md:53 states the SD of a difference of two independent panels is ≈0.016. Without CRN the correct divisor is 0.016 (+0.022 ≈ 1.4 SD, not 2.0). IDEAS.md:1381 item (4) further concedes that 8-episode probe variance exceeds the base-panel SD (recipe-choice variance).\n\nWhere the notes partly address it: REVIEW_PACK.md:53-54, 105-108, 113, 131 label the v6/v6.1-era panels \"unseeded\" and \"not common-random\", and main.tex:232 says so for the v6.1 result; PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:39 lists \"nondeterminism\" generically among limitations; EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:44 notes trainer nondeterminism. None of this covers the R2/R3/RP/R4 lives the paper will report, which EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:6 and certification_report_draft.md:30 describe simply as \"seeded 777\".\n\nInaccuracies in the finding (do not change the verdict): \"unlogged random variable\" is wrong — the brief is persisted at `sleep_XXXX/waking_brief.txt` (sleep_compile.py:171-172) and embedded in every ledger thought row's `prompt` field (batch_loop.py:47); it is unseeded, not unlogged. \"Silently drop the seed\" is imprecise at the sleep call sites, which pass no seed at all; the defect is that `__call__` ignores `seed` and no sleep THINK-call is seeded. Probes use the constant BOOTSTRAP (run_life_v2.py:57-59), so the brief's randomness enters training data and wake context, not the probe prompts — the finding does not claim otherwise.\n\nSeverity stays major, not fatal: the headline cell counts (69/82 positive, 0 harmful in gated pairs) do not depend on CRN, and the fix is small (forward `seed` in `__call__`, freeze or omit `alive` in probe prompts, replace \"common random generation\" in main.tex:142 with \"seeded, not common-random\", and re-express \"≈ k SD\" statements against the difference SD 0.016). But an already-drafted false methods claim, a √2 overstatement in every paired-significance statement, unseeded generated text inside the training corpus, and a defect flagged three times on 2026-09-06 and left half-fixed are more than minor.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}

Codex flagged the dropped seed on 2026-09-06 (research_loop/advisory/20260906_fable_v61_longrun_independent_audit_v1.md:275; 20260906_parenting_headline_implementation_gap_audit_v1.md:172) and only `batch()` was fixed. The finding is stronger than stated: train_adapter.py (the v1 trainer every R2/R3/R4/RP life uses per COORDINATION.md:810 and the Codex path audit at :568-590) has no seed at all and uses lora_dropout=0.05 — the `--seed` fix credited in EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:20 exists only in train_adapter_v21.py:31-40; the parent brief (parent_brief.py:228 via parent_backend.py:162-170) is an unseeded temperature-0.4 sample shown at every wake (run_life_v2.py:135-145); probe_adapter.py:34-35 passes no gen_seed despite COORDINATION.md:337 calling those rank-cal panels \"seeded\"; and the nine-rep noise ruler (noise_probes.py:16,37-39) is single-stream, unseeded, default budget 24, whereas life probes are batched, seed 777, budget 16 — REVIEW_PACK.md:53-54 itself calls the nine panels \"unseeded descriptive repeats, not a substitute for common-random confirmatory coupling.\" The authors have already observed the symptom without the cause: COORDINATION.md:896 reports \"same 8 programs, same seed\" scoring 0.509 at the gate and 0.464 at the next probe. Where the implied CRN benefit actually leaks into paper-feeding numbers is the yardstick: certification_report_draft.md:46 (\"+0.022 ≈ 2.0 base-panel SD\"), COORDINATION.md:240/282/354, the gate rationale at :537 (\"tol 0.02 ≈ 2 base-SD\"), and ONBOARDING_ABSTRACT.md:13 (\"noise floor ~1 point\") all divide a difference of two panels by the single-panel SD 0.0113; without effective common-random coupling the independent-difference SD is ~0.016 (REVIEW_PACK.md:52), turning \"≈2.0 SD\" into ≈1.4 SD and weakening the \"2–5 points above the plain model\" sentence for lives 501 (+0.016) and 503 (+0.022). Three corrections to the reviewer: the brief is logged (sleep_compile.py:171-172 writes waking_brief.txt) — it is unseeded, not unlogged; probes use the raw BOOTSTRAP not the brief (run_life_v2.py:57-58), so the brief perturbs the life trajectory rather than the probe prompt; and marker files (run_life_v2.py:54-55) prevent re-running a probe, so the randomness cannot be cherry-picked. Severity: not fatal — the headline claims (0 harmful gated pairs vs 9/82 ungated; collapses at −0.186 and 0.270 vs 0.527; 12k-row writes below base 5/5; rehearsal 0.50–1.00; ritual onset windows) rest on replicate lives and effects far outside any plausible noise band, not on CRN variance reduction, and bitwise reproducibility of a 1024-episode vLLM life was never a load-bearing claim. But it is more than cosmetic because the √2 error is live in numbers destined for the paper and in the gate-tolerance rationale, and because the paper-facing files (EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:6, certification_report_draft.md:30) describe probes as \"seeded 777\" while never disclosing that sleep THINK-calls, the parent, and the v1 trainer are unseeded or that wall-clock text is in every prompt. Prior acknowledgment is generic only (PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:39 \"seed variance, nondeterminism\"; REVIEW_PACK.md:53-54/95/105 and ICLR_2027_READINESS_20260906.md:37 for legacy v6/v6.1 runs), which is why this stays major rather than dropping to minor. Required fixes: forward `seed` in `__call__` and seed the sleep/parent/trainer calls (or disclose them as unseeded); freeze or drop `alive` seconds in probe prompts; delete \"common-random\" from run_life_v2.py:24 and batch_loop.py:107-109 and write \"seeded, not common-random\"; remeasure the noise band as a paired-difference band under the exact probe protocol (batched, budget 16, seed 777, ON=OFF=base) or at minimum √2-correct every \"≈ k SD\" statement and the \"noise floor ~1 point\" line; state all of this in the reproducibility statement.",

Three corrections: (1) the 22% is from the seed-9000 rule-game three-arm (n=1, 'harness data, not the child', COORDINATION.md:472), which EVIDENCE_TABLES omits; the 32B arm the paper would cite is RP 402 (EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:29), whose pre-whitelist gym fallback rate was 6/14 v3 briefs (43%) vs 4/16 (25%) for 14B life 401 — a stronger confound but a different number, so 'parent + 22% fallback' would mislabel the gym arm. (2) The pre-registered RP-vs-control ritual-onset comparison (COORDINATION.md:558-565, reported ~14:00 09-10; EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:32) predates both v3 and the whitelist and used v1/v2 briefs logged 'Leak scan clean' (:509, :523) — it does NOT mix filter regimes; what does mix regimes is EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:29 (RP paired probes to 1024 and rehearsal rates 0.50–1.00), which include 4–6 sleeps in 401/402 where the child rehearsed the rule-game fallback every episode — that is the real damage to the permitted claim 'children rehearse parental lessons in context' (PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:21). (3) 'Indistinguishable in the ledger' is overstated: parent_brief.py:242-245 logs hits and text per brief, so regime is inferable, just untagged. Severity stays major, not fatal: the core writer results (R2/R3 gating, dose harm, storage vs extraction) never touch the parent, and PAPER_WRITING_PROMPT.md:21 already forbids 'parenting improves scores'; but the abstract's leak-scan claim materially exceeds the instrument, the rehearsal claim is contaminated, and the internally flagged confound was never resolved or disclosed. Proposed fixes are right once renumbered per arm; the ledger audit for non-whitelisted pass names or ordered sequences cannot be done from the repo (ledgers live on GPU nodes, EVIDENCE_TABLES.md:3) and is the necessary step before Methods says 'leak-scanned'.",
 "corrected_severity": "major"
}
